=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two strings word1 and word2.
A string x is called valid if x can be rearranged to have word2 as a prefix.
Return the total number of valid substrings of word1.
 
Example 1:

Input: word1 = "bcca", word2 = "abc"
Output: 1
Explanation:
The only valid substring is "bcca" which can be rearranged to "abcc" having "abc" as a prefix.

Example 2:

Input: word1 = "abcabc", word2 = "abc"
Output: 10
Explanation:
All the substrings except substrings of size 1 and size 2 are valid.

Example 3:

Input: word1 = "abcabc", word2 = "aaabc"
Output: 0

 
Constraints:

1 <= word1.length <= 10^5
1 <= word2.length <= 10^4
word1 and word2 consist only of lowercase English letters.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def validSubstringCount(self, word1: str, word2: str) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We need count substrings of word1 such that string x (substring) is valid if x can be rearranged to have word2 as prefix. Meaning there exists permutation y of x such that word2 is prefix of y. Since y length = len(x). If len(x) < len(word2), impossible because prefix length > substring length. If len(x) >= len(word2), can we arrange x so that word2 appears as first m chars? We need choose multiset of chars from x for first m positions exactly word2, and remaining arbitrary. Since x multiset exactly all chars. Need word2's multiset be subset of x's multiset. Because if x contains at least counts required for word2, we can place those chars in prefix positions in order word2, fill rest arbitrary. If not, impossible. So condition: For every char c, count_c(x) >= count_c(word2). Count substrings of word1 whose multiset dominates target counts.

This is classic sliding window count substrings with at least target counts. Need count all substrings where for all c count >= need[c]. word1 length 1e5, word2 length 1e4, alphabet 26. Need O(n * alphabet?) fine 2.6e6. Need count total valid substrings.

Given a substring [l,r] valid iff counts >= need. Need count number of pairs (l,r). Sliding window can count minimal right endpoints for each l: for each l, find smallest r such that [l,r] valid. Then all r' >= r up to n-1 valid? Wait if [l,r] valid, extending right adds chars, counts only increase, so yes. Count += n - r. Need compute for l increasing.

Use two-pointer with condition all counts >= need. Maintain window [l,r] inclusive? Need count. Standard: expand r until valid; then for current l, count n-r; then remove word1[l] and l++ and continue if window invalid expand. But if after removing, window may remain valid; then count for new l same r? Need handle.

Let's define need array target counts. Have current counts for window [l, r] inclusive. Need know if valid: all current[c] >= need[c]. Maintain missing = number of chars with current[c] < need[c]. Initially missing = number of positive target chars? Since current=0; for chars with need>0 missing++. For chars need=0 not missing. If before > need, after >= need, missing unchanged. If need=0 no. We can implement.

Let's formalize algorithm:
```
class Solution:
    def validSubstringCount(self, word1: str, word2: str) -> int:
        need = [0]*26
        for ch in word2:
            need[ord(ch)-97] += 1
        missing = sum(1 for x in need if x > 0)
        cnt = [0]*26
        n = len(word1)
        ans = 0
        r = -1
        for l in range(n):
            while missing > 0 and r + 1 < n:
                r += 1
                c = ord(word1[r]) - 97
                cnt[c] += 1
                if cnt[c] == need[c]:
                    missing -= 1
            if missing == 0:
                ans += n - r
            else:
                break
            c = ord(word1[l]) - 97
            cnt[c] -= 1
            if need[c] > 0 and cnt[c] == need[c] - 1:
                missing += 1
        return ans
```

Let's ensure code style:
```
class Solution:
    def validSubstringCount(self, word1: str, word2: str) -> int:
        need = [0] * 26
        for ch in word2:
            need[ord(ch) - ord('a')] += 1

        missing = sum(1 for x in need if x > 0)
        cnt = [0] * 26
        n = len(word1)
        ans = 0
        r = -1

        for l in range(n):
            while missing > 0 and r + 1 < n:
                r += 1
                c = ord(word1[r]) - 97
                cnt[c] += 1
                if cnt[c] == need[c]:
                    missing -= 1

            if missing == 0:
                ans += n - r
            else:
                break

            c = ord(word1[l]) - 97
            cnt[c] -= 1
            if need[c] > 0 and cnt[c] == need[c] - 1:
                missing += 1

        return ans
```
Need check if after break, we don't remove current l. Fine.

l3 while r+1=5 -> c missing0 ans +=6-5=1 remove a missing1
l4 while r+1=6 end missing>0 break. total10. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
final code. Need output code block only. We can include code. But before final, let's ensure no hidden issue with `missing` and `cnt[c] == need[c]` if need[c]=0 and cnt[c] after increment could equal 0? no. Good.

Let's think if there is a possible issue with "word1 = 'abaaa', word2 = 'a'" and if code returns14. Good.

Now, final code.</think>```python
class Solution:
    def validSubstringCount(self, word1: str, word2: str) -> int:
        need = [0] * 26
        for ch in word2:
            need[ord(ch) - 97] += 1

        missing = sum(1 for x in need if x > 0)
        cnt = [0] * 26
        n = len(word1)
        ans = 0
        r = -1

        for l in range(n):
            while missing > 0 and r + 1 < n:
                r += 1
                c = ord(word1[r]) - 97
                cnt[c] += 1
                if cnt[c] == need[c]:
                    missing -= 1

            if missing == 0:
                ans += n - r
            else:
                break

            c = ord(word1[l]) - 97
            cnt[c] -= 1
            if need[c] > 0 and cnt[c] == need[c] - 1:
                missing += 1

        return ans
```
